## Document 006: 10月金融数据点评：社融增速回升

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月金融数据点评：社融增速回升.pdf
Report source: 中证鹏元
Date: 2026-11-24
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

中证鹏元资信评估股份有限公司
研究发展部
赵云蛟
zhaoyj@cspengyuan.com
吴进辉
wujh@cspengyuan.com
主要内容：
11 月 13 日，央行公布 2026 年 10 月金融统计数据。总体来看，10 月社融存量增速
企稳回升，信贷总量持续承压，且单月新增规模转负，广义融资结构重塑趋势延续。
社融增量同比多增，主要依赖政府债券拉动，同时表外及直接融资边际改善，8,000
亿政策性金融工具投放带动相关配套资金加速落地，对委托贷款形成支撑。信贷结
构持续分化，微观主体加杠杆意愿依然偏弱。其中企业中长贷在新型政策性金融工
具集中投放支撑下维持相对韧性，而地产贴息政策效果尚未充分体现，居民端资产
负债表延续收缩。监管强化防范资金空转，大幅压降票据融资。货币与存款方面广
义流动性扩张呈现企稳迹象，前期政策资金陆续拨付至相关实体企业，带动企业活
期现金流改善并推升 M1 增速回升。存款端主要是流动性在季初跨部门转移，同时
政府债集中发行与季节性缴税推动财政存款扩张，对实体流动性产生一定的回笼效

应。
整体而言，10 月金融数据反映出资产负债表的自发修复与收缩进程仍在延续，实
体内生复苏动能尚待巩固，但在政策组合拳显效下，全年 GDP 增长目标预计可顺
利实现。叠加四季度权益市场大幅调整，市场避险情绪被进一步放大，微观主体整
体呈现防御收缩态势。政策层面，预计年内继续出台增量举措概率不高，主要依赖
存量政策效能发挥。后续需重点观察前期落地的地产贴息政策对房地产销售端的实
质效果，持续关注政策性金融工具转化为实物工作量后对企业中长期贷款的带动作
用。货币政策方面，政策利率换锚正式落地相当于释放了隐性降息效果，在配合跨
年流动性平稳过渡或者稳定权益市场等诉求下，仍存在降准的可能性。当前市场博
弈的核心将逐步转向对 2027 年的定调，重点关注 12 月的中央经济工作会议。
社融增速回升——10 月金融数据点评 宏观点评
2026 年 11 月 13 日

### Page 2

表 1 10 月金融数据概览

### Page 3

2026 年 11 月 13 日，央行公布 2026 年 10 月金融统计数据。
一、社融：10 月社融存量增速企稳回升，政府债融资依然是核心支撑
2026 年 10 月新增社融 1.14 万亿元，同比多增 3,251 亿元，社融存量增速较上月回升 0.1 个百分点至
7.2%。本月社融总量整体企稳回升，广义融资结构重塑趋势仍在延续。在信贷总量承压的背景下，社融增量
主要依赖政府债券拉动。直接融资累计占比达 53%，宏观融资大盘正逐步降低对传统表内信贷的单一依赖，
政策驱动下的融资结构转型特征持续显现。
分项数据来看，政府债券当月新增 1.18 万亿元，同比多增 6,983 亿元，政府债发行规模在 9 月冲高后
虽有所回落，但仍然同比多增，在化债与稳增长诉求下构成广义融资的核心增量。人民币贷款减少 5,846 亿
元，同比多减 5,692 亿元，实体信贷需求不足依然是社融的主要拖累项。表外及直接融资方面边际改善，委
托贷款当月新增 1,862 亿元，同比多增 208 亿元，10 月底，8,000 亿政策性金融工具全部投放，带动相关配

套资金加速落地，对委托贷款形成支撑。表外未贴现承兑汇票减少 1,687 亿元，同比少减 1,208 亿元，同时
企业债券新增 3,446 亿元，同比多增 946 亿元，在融资环境整体宽松及利率下行周期中实体企业直接债券融
资意愿整体回升。
图1 广义融资结构重塑延续，人民币信贷是主要拖累，政府债是主要支撑
资料来源：Wind，中证鹏元整理

二、信贷：10 月信贷总量承压，政策发力支撑企业中长端维持相对韧性
10 月新增人民币贷款减少 1,000 亿元，同比多减 3,200 亿元。信贷总量相对承压且各部门结构持续分
化，微观主体加杠杆意愿依然偏弱，资产负债表的自发修复与收缩进程仍在延续。当前信贷扩张的动能主要
依赖宏观政策的托底，实体经济内生性的投资与消费需求尚待进一步激发。
居民端信贷当月减少 4,300 亿元，同比多减 696 亿元。其中居民短贷减少 2,700 亿元，同比少减 166 亿
元，尽管 10 月已进入双十一大促阶段，且各地消费品以旧换新及相关贴息政策正陆续落地发力，但实际消
费信贷的整体回升态势依然偏弱，微观主体在收入预期不稳背景下对日常消费加杠杆的意愿普遍审慎，消
费信用扩张动能受到实质性制约。居民中长贷减少 1,563 亿元，同比多减 863 亿元，主要受制于房地产市场
销售端仍在底部调整区间，新增按揭贷款的派生规模有限，10 月开始实施的地产贴息政策对信贷数据的拉

动尚未充分体现，多重因素共同拖累居民端资产负债表扩张。
企业端贷款减少 1,600 亿元，同比多减 5,100 亿元，内部呈现长强短弱特征。企业中长贷新增 1,600 亿
元，同比多增 1,300 亿元，在信贷整体偏弱背景下表现出相对韧性，主要反映了政策端的支撑效果，新型政
策性金融工具在 9 月-10 月集中投放，为重大项目有效补充了资本金，叠加大规模设备更新等产业政策的结
构性引导，直接撬动了银行长端配套信贷的定向投放。企业短贷新增 100 亿元，同比多增 2,000 亿元，实体
企业常规营运资金需求边际企稳。票据融资大幅减少 3,117 亿元，同比多减 8,123 亿元，主要由于监管持续
强化防范资金空转套利，银行通过票据贴现单纯做大信贷规模的行为受到严格规范。近期市场关于票据剥
离信贷属性的预期有所升温，未来若相关统计口径优化落地，票据定价及业务模式将进一步脱离信贷扰动
并回归真实贸易本源。

资料来源：Wind，中证鹏元整理
三、货币与存款：10 月流动性季节性转移，财政存款扩张，M1 增速边际回升
10 月 M1 同比 4.4%，较 9 月回升 1.2 个百分点，M2 同比 7.1%，较 9 月微降 0.1 个百分点。同时社融
存量同比 7.2%，较上月回升 0.1 个百分点。整体看，10 月广义流动性扩张呈现企稳迹象，资金活化程度边
际改善，而存款端则表现出典型的季初跨部门转移及缴税抽水效应。
从货币供给走势看，M1 回升与 M2 微降带动 M2 与 M1 剪刀差由上月的 4.0%收窄至 2.7%。M2 偏弱主
要由表内信贷收缩导致存款派生机制受阻所致。M1 增速的回升主要来源于前期宏观政策资金逐步向下游传
导，9 月至 10 月集中投放的新型政策性金融工具资金以及前期发行的政府债券资金陆续拨付至相关实体企
业的项目账户，直接带动了企业活期现金流的边际改善。社融增速回升且与 M2 剪刀差间隔 10 个月转正至

0.1%，进一步确认广义流动性扩张的企稳态势。

### Page 8

存款方面，10 月新增人民币存款 1.21 万亿元，同比多增 6,000 亿元，主要是季初流动性跨部门转移。
居民存款减少 9,200 亿元，同比少减 4,200 亿元，企业存款减少 1.25 万亿元，同比多减 1,647 亿元。与实体
部门存款减少相对应，非银机构存款增加 1.58 万亿元，同比少增 2,700 亿元，非银资金规模的扩张主要受
季初理财资金回流非银体系拉动。此外 10 月财政存款增加 1.4 万亿元，同比多增 6,800 亿元，缴税大月，
叠加政府债集中发行导致财政存款账面大幅扩张，财政资金阶段性沉淀对实体流动性形成一定的回笼效应，
后续财政资金能否加速下拨将关系到 M1 增速回升的持续性。
图11 季初流动性跨部门转移，财政存款大幅扩张

总体来看，10 月社融存量增速企稳回升，信贷总量持续承压，且单月新增规模转负，广义融资结构重
塑趋势延续。社融增量同比多增，主要依赖政府债券拉动，同时表外及直接融资边际改善，8,000 亿政策性
金融工具投放带动相关配套资金加速落地，对委托贷款形成支撑。信贷结构持续分化，微观主体加杠杆意愿
依然偏弱。其中企业中长贷在新型政策性金融工具集中投放支撑下维持相对韧性，而地产贴息政策效果尚
未充分体现，居民端资产负债表延续收缩。监管强化防范资金空转，大幅压降票据融资。货币与存款方面广
义流动性扩张呈现企稳迹象，前期政策资金陆续拨付至相关实体企业，带动企业活期现金流改善并推升 M1
增速回升。存款端主要是流动性在季初跨部门转移，同时政府债集中发行与季节性缴税推动财政存款扩张，
对实体流动性产生一定的回笼效应。
整体而言，10 月金融数据反映出资产负债表的自发修复与收缩进程仍在延续，实体内生复苏动能尚待
巩固，但在政策组合拳显效下，全年 GDP 增长目标预计可顺利实现。叠加四季度权益市场大幅调整，市场

避险情绪被进一步放大，微观主体整体呈现防御收缩态势。政策层面，预计年内继续出台增量举措概率不
高，主要依赖存量政策效能发挥。后续需重点观察前期落地的地产贴息政策对房地产销售端的实质效果，持
续关注政策性金融工具转化为实物工作量后对企业中长期贷款的带动作用。货币政策方面，政策利率换锚
正式落地相当于释放了隐性降息效果，在配合跨年流动性平稳过渡或者稳定权益市场等诉求下，仍存在降
准的可能性。当前市场博弈的核心将逐步转向对 2027 年的定调，重点关注 12 月的中央经济工作会议。

## Document 034: 20261027_投資週報_AI資金壓力擾動市場，科技基本面仍穩健

### Page 1

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 27 日
上週重點訊息
◼ AI 投資回報疑慮升溫，資金轉向債市避險：市場對大型科技企業 AI 基礎建設投資規模、融資
需求及未來獲利回收能力的疑慮升溫，導致科技股賣壓擴大，10 月 22 日 Nasdaq 指數下跌
約 2.6%。隨著市場風險偏好下降，避險資金轉向美國公債，10 年期公債殖利率單日下降約 19
個基點，創 2023 年 3 月以來最大單日跌幅，並於 23 日回升至 4.99%。市場關注焦點逐漸由
AI 投資規模轉向資本支出效益，後續大型科技企業財報及現金流表現，將成為評估 AI 產業評

價合理性的重要依據。
◼ 中東衝突推升通膨風險：中東衝突持續升溫，伊朗支持的葉門胡塞武裝攻擊沙烏地阿拉伯，引
發市場對原油供應中斷的擔憂，推升布蘭特原油價格於 10 月 23 日升至每桶 116.72 美元，單
週上漲約 6.2%，創 2022 年以來最高週收盤水準。能源價格攀升加劇全球通膨壓力，並壓抑
市場風險偏好。
◼ 法國政治動盪加劇財政疑慮，歐洲主權債風險重新受到關注：法國因生活成本上升爆發大規模
抗議，進一步加深市場對政府財政整頓能力的疑慮。10 月 19 日法國 10 年期公債殖利率上升
約 10 個基點至 4.88%附近，與德國公債利差擴大至約 144 個基點，創 2012 年以來最高收盤
水準，法股單日下跌約 1.5%，歐元同步走弱。市場擔憂政治不確定性可能削弱財政改革進度，
並透過主權債評價影響銀行業資產負債表。後續須關注法國財政政策、歐洲主權債利差及銀行
融資成本變化。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短線宜審慎，中期聚焦基本面穩健的 AI 企業：近期 AI 資本支出、融資需求及評價調整

使科技股波動加劇，但先進製程與半導體設備需求仍具韌性，AI 中長期成長趨勢未改變。隨市
場焦點由成長題材轉向投資效益，企業營收與訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率的重要性提
升，具技術優勢、財務穩健及獲利能見度的企業相對有利，而高度依賴外部融資者宜審慎看待。
◼ 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：市場風險偏好降溫時，美國公債仍具一定
避險效果。儘管 Fed 貨幣政策路徑與能源價格增添利率不確定性，短天期債券易受政策利率
牽動，超長天期債券面臨財政赤字、國債供給及期限溢價等壓力，相較之下，中天期美債的風
險報酬較為均衡。信用債方面，投資等級企業基本面及現金流仍具韌性，後續仍可持續關注。

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 11 月 23 日

1 群益投顧 
研究員：葉芷瑄 vanessa.yeh@cim.capital.com.tw 
前 日 收 盤 價  2 9 6 . 00 元  
目標價

國巨*(2327 TT) Trading Buy

短 期 營 運 依 然 強 勁，惟 AI 不 確 定 性 加 劇，評 價 面 臨 調 節 壓
力 ，投資建議為 Trading Buy 
投 資 建 議 ： 展望未來，國巨 3Q26 營 運 表 現 亮 眼 ， 4Q26 將 持 續
增 長，惟 800V 遞 延 加 上 AI 不 確 定 性 加 劇，評 價 面 臨 調 節 壓 力，
因此給予 Trading Buy 的 投 資 建 議 。  
3Q26 財 報 表 現 優異，毛 利 率 顯 著 提 升：國巨 3Q26 營收達 495.32
億元（QoQ +11.4%、 YoY +49.7% ），主 因 受 惠 AI 伺 服 器 需 求
強勁； 產 品 組 合 優 化 與 高 毛 利 產 品 貢 獻 提 升 ， 單 季 毛 利 率 攀 升
至 41.00%（ QoQ +2.50ppts 、 YoY +4.81ppts ） ； 費 用 率 11.52%
（ QoQ -0.56ppts、YoY -1.82ppts）；營 業 利 益 146.03 億 元（ QoQ 
+24.3%、 YoY +93.1% ）， 單 季 EPS 5.76 元 ， 整 體 獲 利 表 現 強 勁
並 優 於 預 期 。  
預估 4Q26 營 運 微 幅 季 增 ， BB Ratio 略 為 下 滑 ：  BBR 方 面 ，
3Q26 將近 2（ 2Q26 為 2.2） ， 下 滑 並 非 因 為 訂 單 減 少 （ 目 前 訂
單 持 續 增 加 ， 訂 單 能 見 度 達 半 年 以 上 ） ， 加 上 高 階 產 品 比 重 提
升 ， 預 估 國巨 4Q26 營 收 與 毛 利 率 皆 預 期 微 幅 成 長 。 綜 上 所 述 ，
預估 2026 年 EPS 20.52 元 ， 獲 利 呈 現 大 幅 提 升 。  
展望 2027 年 AI 持 續 強 勁，惟 800V 遞 延 影 響 大：展望 2027 年，
除消費性電子外 ， 其 餘 產 品 線 與 應 用 皆 預 期 成 長 ， 營 收 將 逐 季
向上， 主 要 成 長 引 擎 為 MLCC 與 鉭 電 容 ，2027 年 全年維 持 正 向
看 法。產 能 方 面，3Q26 整 體 產 能 利 用 率 達 90%，2027 年 已 核 可
擴產 10%至 15%， 集 中 於 MLCC 與 鉭 電 容 ， 自 2Q27 開出 3%、
3Q27 與 4Q27 各 開 出 6%， 合 計 15%。惟 800V 遞 延 影 響 大 ， 市
場 較 原 先 預 估 更 為 樂 觀 的 獲 利 成 長 預 期 有 所 下 修，預 估 2027 年
EPS 31.48 元。

2 群益投顧 
  ESG：  
國 巨 致 力 於 落 實 企 業 永 續 發 展 ， 在 環 境 方 面 持 續 推 動 綠 色 製 造 、 節 能 減 碳 、
廢棄物減量與供應鏈永續管理；社會方面重視員工職涯發展、職場安全衛
生、多 元 包 容 與 社 會 公 益 參 與；公 司 治 理 方 面 持 續 強 化 董 事 會 獨 立 性、資 訊
揭 露 透 明 度 ， 以 提 升 企 業 經 營 韌 性 與 長 期 競 爭 力 。

公 司 簡 介 ：  
國巨（2327 TT） 成 立 於  1977 年 ， 現 今 為 全 球 第 一 大 晶 片 電 阻 （ R-Chip）
製 造 商 、第 一 大 鉭 質 電 容（ Tantalum Capacitor ）製 造 商 、第 三 大 積 層 陶 瓷 電
容（ MLCC）及 第 三 大 電 感 元 件（ Inductors）供 應 商 。 公 司 產 品 瞄 準  AI、汽
車 、 工 業 、 5G 與  IoT、 醫 療 、 航 太 、 智 慧 型 手 機 、 PC、 電 源 管 理 及 綠 電 能
等 市 場 ，主 要 品 牌 為  YAGEO、KEMET、PULSE，產 品 線 涵 蓋 磁 性 元 件 、鉭
電容、MLCC、 電 阻 、 感 測 器 及 其 他 產 品 。  
• 3Q26 產 品 別 營 收 佔 比 ：以鉭 電 容 佔  23.7% 最高（自 2Q26 起取代 
MLCC 成 為 第 一 大 部 門 ） ， 磁 性 元 件 佔  22.3% 次之， MLCC 佔  
21.5%， 電 阻 佔  15.3%，感測器佔 10.6%，其他佔 6.6%。  
• 3Q26 終 端 應 用 別 佔 比 ：工業應用佔 28.7% 最 高 ， 運 算 與 企 業 系 統
佔  28.3%，車用佔 14.9% ，電信佔 12.5%，消費性佔 11.7% ，航太
／ 國 防 ／ 醫 療 佔  3.9%（其中 AI 相 關 營 收 佔 比 已 達  19.8%）。 
• 3Q26 區 域 與 通 路 佔 比 ： 區 域 別 以 美 國  27.1%、 中 國  27.0%、 亞 太 其
他地區 26.4%、歐 洲  19.5% 分 布；銷 售 通 路 以 經 銷 商  48.9% 最 高，
OEM 佔  32.4%， EMS 佔  18.7%。

3 群益投顧 
表 一 、 國 巨 應 用 別

資 料 來 源 ： 國巨， 群 益 投 顧 彙 整  
3Q26 財 報 表 現 優異，毛 利 率 顯 著 提 升：國巨 3Q26 營 收 達 495.32 億 元（ QoQ 
+11.4%、YoY +49.7%）， 主 因 受 惠 AI 伺 服 器 需 求 強 勁 ； 產 品 組 合 優 化 與 高
毛 利 產 品 貢 獻 提 升 ， 單 季 毛 利 率 攀 升 至 41.00% （ QoQ +2.50ppts 、 YoY 
+4.81ppts） ； 費 用 率 11.52%（ QoQ -0.56ppts、 YoY -1.82ppts） ； 營 業 利 益
146.03 億元（QoQ +24.3% 、 YoY +93.1% ） ； 稅 後 淨 利 119.81 億元（QoQ 
+27.2%、YoY +88.5% ），單 季 EPS 5.76 元，整 體 獲 利 表 現 強 勁 並 優 於 預 期。 
預估 4Q26 營 運 微 幅 季 增，BB Ratio 略 為 下 滑：透 過 產 品 組 合 優 化 與 費 用 控
管 追 求 規 模 經 濟 並 帶 動 淨 利 提 升 。 以 2Q26 毛利率 38.5%為 基 準 ， 鉭 電 容 、
電 阻 與 感 測 器 高 於 公 司 平 均，磁 性 元 件 與 平 均 相 當，MLCC 則 低 於 平 均，惟
3Q26 MLCC 毛 利 率 已 明 顯 貼 近 平 均 水 準 ， 不過稼 動 率 拉 升 、 AI 貢 獻 增 加 及
成 本 控 管 見 效 ， 使 毛 利 率 有 望 持 續 向 上 。 BBR 方 面 ， 3Q26 將近 2（ 2Q26 為

4 群益投顧 
2.2），下滑並非因為訂單減少（目前訂單持續增加，訂單能見度達半年以
上）， 加 上 高 階 產 品 比 重 提 升 ， 預估國巨 4Q26 營 收 與 毛 利 率 皆 預 期 微 幅 成
長 。 綜 上 所 述 ， 預 估 2026 年 EPS 20.52 元 ， 獲 利 呈 現 大 幅 提 升 。  
展望 2027 年 AI 持 續 強 勁 ， 惟 800V 遞 延 影 響 大 ： 展望 2027 年 ， 除 消 費 性
電子外，其 餘 產 品 線 與 應 用 皆 預 期 成 長，營 收 將 逐 季 向 上，主 要 成 長 引 擎 為
MLCC 與 鉭 電 容 ， 排除 AI 最 保 守 的 情 況 下 ， 營 收 至 少 有 20%以 上 的 增 長 ，
2027 年 全年維 持 正 向 看 法 。 產 能 方 面 ， 3Q26 整 體 產 能 利 用 率 達 90%， 高 雄
大 發 三 廠 稼 動 率 已 從 2026 年 初 的 70%拉 升 至 滿 載；2027 年 已 核 可 擴 產 10%
至 15%， 集 中 於 MLCC 與 鉭 電 容 ， 自 2Q27 開出 3%、 3Q27 與 4Q27 各 開 出
6%，合 計 15%。惟 800V 遞 延 影 響 大，市 場 較 原 先 預 估 更 為 樂 觀 的 獲 利 成 長
預 期 有 所 下 修 ， 預 估 2027 年 EPS 31.48 元。 
AI 雜 音 升 溫 ， 2028 年 AI 基 建 遞 延 ， MLCC 供 需 逐 步 均 衡 ：  
隨 著 市 場 對 AI 資 本 支 出 與 基 建 進 度 的 雜 音 升 溫 ， 若 2028 年 AI 基 建 出 現 遞
延，MLCC 整 體 供 需 結 構 可 能 逐 步 走 向 均 衡，減 緩 極 端 供 需 失 衡 所 引 發 的 劇
烈 波 動 。 對 國 巨 而 言 ， 相 關 漲 價 可 能 趨 緩 ， 毛 利 率 增 長 趨 勢 預 估 於 2028 年
見頂 ， 加 上 消 費 電 子 需 求 放 緩 影 響 ， 預 估 2028 年 成 長 展 望 較 為 平 緩 。

投 資 建 議：展 望 未 來，國 巨 3Q26 營 運 表 現 亮 眼，4Q26 將 持 續 增 長，惟 800V
遞 延 加 上 AI 不 確 定 性 加 劇 ， 評 價 面 臨 調 節 壓 力 ， 因 此 給 予 Trading Buy 的
投 資 建 議 。

Forward PE Band

8 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】  
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 ≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <15%

中立(Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等  
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整  
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】  
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較 3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為 四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等 係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。   
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」 及 「 區 間 操 作 」 均有 upside 目 標 價 。 差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。  
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ：  
1. 達 目 標 價 。  
2. 雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。  
3. 推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】 
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

 投資評價與建議 
維持買進，目標價降至 358 元：(1) 3Q26 獲利表現不如預期，主因 PCB 比重拉升
及面對中國在地價格競爭，(2) 能見度高，但隨企業 AI Server 帶動 HDI/HLC PCB 
與 ABF 載板 2H26-2027 年接力放量，短期獲利改善有限。我們預估 2026/ 2027
年 EPS 為 14.01/19.92 元，目標 PE 則由 30x 下修至 18x，反映市場對設備類股評
價的調整，目標價隨之下修到 358 元(18x 2027 EPSF)。

### Page 2

2

志聖以光與熱為核心，發展熱處理平台：志聖成立於 1966 年，目前資本額 16.2 億
新台幣，為 G2C 集團當中的核心成員之一，以紫外光、烘烤、壓膜塗佈、電漿製
程等技術提供相關壓膜、貼、撕膜、烘烤、電鍍相關設備。公司初期以 PCB 設備
起家，而後伴隨台灣電子產業升級，切入半導體、組裝、面板、太陽能等產業，近
年陸續在 IC 載板、高階 HDI、先進封裝、HBM 等高附加價值市場發光發熱。公司
也與均豪、均華、東捷組成 G2C+聯盟，共同實現台灣先進封裝在地化。志聖主要
生產據點位於台灣林口、台中、中國廣州、昆山，並在台南、廣東、泰國、美國鳳
凰城有服務據點。1-3Q26 營收以產業別劃分，半導體佔約 45%、先進 PCB 28%、
傳統 PCB 21%、其他電子 4%、FPD 3%。主要競爭對手包含 EVG、TOK、SUSS、
Yield Engineering Systems(YES)等。客戶包含台積電、日月光、臻鼎、力成、
Amkor、Micron、欣興、勝宏等電子中上游廠商。 
3Q26 獲利不如預期：志聖 3Q26 營收 29.8 億(+7.0%QoQ，+93.9%YoY)，毛利
率 43.3%，稅後淨利 5.5 億(-13.4%QoQ，+162.1%YoY)，EPS 3.51 元。毛利率
表現不如預期主因出貨較多中國 PCB 用低階設備，使產品組合轉差，即半導體設
備/PCB 設備由 1H26 的 46%/46%變動至 1-3Q26 的 45%/49%，營業費用亦隨
獎酬增加及研發專案數的提升而高於預期。 
能見度高，但我們對獲利趨於審慎：管理層預期 2026 年底在手訂單約 100-110
億，較去年同期約 43 億元倍增，其中半導體佔比約 53%以上(以 OSAT 能見度較
高)、高階 PCB 35-37%。值得注意的是，半導體設備交期已由 4-10 個月拉長至 4-
13 個月；PCB 設備交期則約 3-8 個月，主因 OSAT 在廠房未完工前即下單，以及
上游零組件(機械手臂)交期的拉長所致，志聖持續以 G2C+聯盟和日系供應商溝通
並聯合採購，目前風險可控。展望後市，我們預期在晶圓廠先進封裝產能建置外溢
的環境下，志聖將透過挑單生產，降低傳統 PCB 接單比例以維持獲利率穩健成長。 
載板挖槽嵌入製程，2028 年再添動能：隨 AI 晶片尺寸持續放大，台系載板廠正
發展新的生產方式，以降低整片翹曲同時達到微縮線寬/線距的目的。志聖在此製
程中提供背面貼膜，以及 ABF 絕緣膜的壓合與撕膜設備。由於層數倍增將使壓合
與撕膜次數同步增加，加上載具尺寸放大推升單機價值，我們認為這將是志聖 
2028 年後載板設備的重要成長來源。 
財務預估：研究部預估志聖 2026 年營收 111.1 億元(+82.2%YoY)，毛利率 46.3%，
稅後淨利 22.7 億元(+173.0%YoY)，EPS 14.01 元(原估 16.94 元，下修主因本業
獲利表現不如預期及股本膨脹)。展望 2027 年，公司尚未給予明確展望，我們考量
短期 PCB/HLC 急單湧現壓抑毛利率，故下修獲利預估，預期 2027 年營收 148.4
億元(+33.5%YoY)，毛利率 46.9%，稅後淨利 32.2 億元(+42.2%YoY)，EPS 19.92
元(原估 25.15 元，下修主因獲利預估調降及股本膨脹)。

## Document 119: 261115_ms_cotech

Source PDF: all report pdf/261115_ms_cotech.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-15
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

M
Update
Co-Tech Development Corp.  | Asia Pacific
Key Japanese Competitor
Announced Price Hikes
What's new? Our supply chain checks suggest that one of Co-Tech's key Japanese
competitors notified customers earlier this week of price increases for its
electrodeposited (ED) copper foil products. The company will raise prices by 30%
for ¼ oz (9µm) and ⅓ oz (12µm) ED copper foils and by 15% for other thicknesses,
effective for shipments from December 2026 onward. This aligns with our previous

supply chain check findings published in our Nov 4 report (link).
Our view: The increases extend beyond high-end HVLP products to copper foils
used in consumer electronics. We believe this reflects rising production costs and,
more importantly, tightening supply, with positive implications for Co-Tech. We
expect Co-Tech to follow with further price increases, although likely at smaller
magnitudes given the ~10% hike it implemented in October.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+

Rachel L Zhang

Key Takeaways
Earlier this week, a key Japanese competitor issued a price increase notice to its
customers for electrodeposited copper foils.
This aligns with our  supply chain check findings published in our Nov 4 report.
We believe the  price hikes have positive implications for Co-Tech and expect Co-
Tech to follow with price increases as well.

However, since Co-Tech already implemented a ~10% price hike in October, we
don't expect it to announce a price hike of the same magnitude.
November 15, 2026 08:52 PM GMT

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

2026.11.13 
洪有辰 C00270@ibfs.com.tw 
3090.TT 日電貿 
 庫存等待進一步消化，日正當中再續衝

[財務資訊更新，法說會更新]   
   公司簡介 
日電貿成立於 1993 年，為台灣規模最大的被動元件代理商，其代理的企業品牌涵蓋
美國、日本與韓國等多家知名被動元件與電子零組件公司，產品線以各種電容、電感、
半導體元件與其他光電元件等為主，近期則開始聚焦於高規格電容類產品。 
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
買進 172 
【前次投資建議】強力買進，256 
【大盤指數/股價】43092 / 132.5

➢ 因業外減少，3Q26 EPS 降至 2.46 元，低於投研部預期 
3Q26 營收 58.61 億元，QoQ+13.7%、YoY+44.7%；受惠 AI 伺服器及
資料中心需求強勁，帶動 MLCC 等高價值產品出貨成長，毛利率提升至
21.12%，QoQ+3ppts，YoY+5.2ppts；EPS 2.49 元，獲利呈現季衰退
並低於投研部預期，主因為 2Q26 存在一筆業外股利收入 4.5 億元。 
➢ 4Q26 客戶放緩拉貨節奏，且大客戶佔比上升，營收與獲利續降 
受惠 AI 高階 MLCC 供需緊俏，以及日韓原廠產能轉移，部分消費性產
品交期已延長至 24～36 週，預期 4Q26 AI 與一般型產品報價同步調漲，
MLCC 營收占比可望由 52%升至 54%。預估 4Q26 營收 58.17 億元，
QoQ-0.8% 、 YoY+52.5% ；毛利率 20.64% ， QoQ-0.5ppts ，
YoY+2.7ppts；EPS 2.40 元。惟 2026 年底客戶控管庫存，加上大型客
戶議價能力較強，獲利率略受稀釋。 
➢ 供需失衡延續至 2027 年，漲價潮與 AI 占比破六成將使獲利再升 
日電貿因輝達 Vera Rubin 平台出貨遞延，客戶拉貨速度低於預期，導致
3Q26 存貨大幅攀升至 46.03 億元，主要反映高階元件 ASP 上升及長交
期產品備貨需求，惟隨 2027 年初 Rubin 放量，庫存可望逐步去化。展
望 2027 年，AI 與傳統伺服器需求同步成長，高階 MLCC 因製程難度
高、良率偏低，供給持續緊俏，預估 MLCC 營收占比升至 57%，年增
49.7%；固態電容及電解電容營收亦分別成長 44.7%及 34.9%。隨著 AI
營收占比提升至約 60%，加上原廠漲價及產品組合優化，毛利率有望重
拾上漲動能。 
➢ 預估 2027 年 EPS 14.37 元，給予買進評等，目標價 172 元 
投研部預估日電貿於 2026 年/2027 年營收 213.24 億元/304.43 億元，
YoY+35.58%/+42.76% ； 毛 利 率 19.99%/22.15% ， YoY+3.6ppts 
/2.2ppts；稅後 EPS 9.80 元/14.37 元，目前 PER 約在 13.5X/9.2X，參
考過去五年 PER 區間大部分在 10X~18X，目前股價以 2027 年衡量低
於區間下緣，考量公司獨家代理的多項高階產品因供需持續失衡而有望
漲價，使 AI 佔比持續上升，故給予買進評等，但下修目標價至 172 元
(2027F PE 12X)。

### Page 2

- 2 -

個股報告

3Q26 因業外減少，EPS 降至 2.49 元，低於投研部預期 
日電貿 3Q26 營收 58.61 億元，QoQ+13.7%、YoY+44.7%，主因為 AI 伺服器、資
料中心及電源相關需求同步增加，帶動陶瓷電容(MLCC)等高附加價值產品出貨成長，
以營收結構來看，公司三大類別分別為 MLCC 佔 52%、固態電容(SCP)佔 26%、電解
電容(ECP)佔 9%。此外，SCP 佔比下滑至 26%，因客戶擔憂缺貨而於 2Q26 提前備
貨，加上 3Q26 MLCC 成長速度更快所致；毛利率提升至 21.12%，QoQ+3ppts，
YoY+5.2ppts，呈現出 AI 伺服器與資料中心需求強勁，帶動 MLCC 與各類電容產品
出貨上升的現象；然而稅前淨利 8.98 億元，QoQ-13.3%，主因為 2Q26 存在一筆業
外股利收入 4.5 億元；EPS 2.49 元，QoQ-22.4%，YoY+26.4%。3Q26 營收低於投
研部預期，因公司客戶於 2Q26 提前備貨，故 3Q26 季增幅回歸較穩定狀態；獲利亦
因上述的業外減少而低於預期。累計前三季 EPS 7.4 元，YoY+76.2%。 
圖 1：日電貿產品組合佔比預估

資料來源：國票投顧整理 
2026 年底客戶放緩拉貨節奏，且大客戶佔比上升，4Q26 營收與獲利續降 
日電貿目前因 AI 用的高階 MLCC 供需緊俏，一般型產品也受到日韓原廠產能轉移影
響，部分消費性產品交期已拉長至 24~36 週，預期 4Q26 公司旗下 AI 與一般型料號
報價將調漲，帶動相關營收隨之上升，因此 MLCC 比重有望由 52%進一步升至 54%。
投研部預估 4Q26 營收 58.17 億元，QoQ-0.8%，YoY+52.5%，季衰退的原因推測為
公司客戶於 2026 年底進行盤點並控管庫存，故傾向放緩拉貨腳步；毛利率 20.64%，
QoQ-0.5ppts，YoY+2.7ppts，儘管 AI 占比將超過 50%，但大型客戶占比也持續增
加，公司對這些客戶的產品議價與通路利潤率相對較低，故毛利率有所稀釋；營業利
益率 13.97%，QoQ-0.85ppts，YoY+3.2ppts；稅後 EPS 2.40 元，QoQ-3.5%，
YoY+89.3%。 
MLCC 供需失衡延續至 2027 年，漲價潮與 AI 占比破六成將使獲利再升 
目前輝達(NVDA.US) Vera Rubin 平台出貨遞延，使日電貿的客戶拉貨速度低於預期，
因此在 3Q26 季底累積的庫存金額高達 46.03 億元，且高階元件 ASP 上升，再加上
這些產品的交期較長而需建立更安全的庫存水位，因此相較過往才會異常飆高。公司
認為未來一至兩季需求仍佳，待 Rubin 於 2027 年初開始大量拉貨後，庫存水位可望
下降並轉化為營收，同時存貨週轉天數也從目前的 86 天逐季下降。 
過去兩年，AI 伺服器市場快速成長，傳統伺服器需求則相對溫和；然而，隨著企業 IT

### Page 3

- 3 -

個股報告 
支出回溫與 CSP 大廠持續擴大資本支出，預期自 2026 年~2027 年，傳統伺服器與
AI 伺服器將同步成長，帶動整體伺服器產業規模持續擴大。就市場規模而言，全球伺
服器於 2027 年預估約達 1.1 兆美元，並於 2028 年再提升至 1.6 兆美元，年增幅約
15%。其中，輝達 NVL72 機櫃將成為重要成長動能，2028 年相關市場產值可達約 1
兆美元，占整體市場約 63%；另一方面，ASIC 伺服器近年出貨量快速增加，預估至
2028 年市場滲透率將提升至 65%，顯示雲端業者正逐步擴大自研晶片的部署。從晶
片出貨量來看，2025 年 ASIC 約為 430 萬顆，輝達與 AMD 相關 GPU 則約 550 萬
顆；至 2028 年，ASIC 出貨量預估將增至 2,220 萬顆，較 2025 年成長約 5.2 倍，
GPU 相關出貨量則可達 1,610 萬顆，成長約 2.9 倍。整體而言，未來伺服器市場將由
傳統伺服器復甦、AI 訓練與推論需求擴張，以及 ASIC 自研晶片加速滲透等多重因素
共同推動，顯示 GPU 與 ASIC 兩大運算架構將呈現同步成長，而非單純的替代關係。 
圖 2：日電貿存貨金額 單位：億元

資料來源：國票投顧整理 
AI 伺服器所需的高階 MLCC，因產品規格涉及高容值、高耐壓與高可靠度要求，製程
難度明顯高於一般規格產品，導致產品良率相對偏低。相較一般 MLCC 良率可達
99.9%，部分 AI 高階產品良率依產品別與不同客戶的設計差異約落在 40%~80%不
等，使生產相同數量的合格品，需投入更多製造資源，因此形成較大的產能消耗。儘
管如此，受惠 AI 伺服器與高效能運算需求持續擴張，高階 MLCC 訂單仍相當充足，
供給短期內不易快速增加。日電貿目前客戶需求都維持強勁，尤其 MLCC 產品拉貨動
能明確，因此維持緊俏的狀況將延續至 2027 年，使其成為被動元件產業中具備較高
成長潛力的重要產品。投研部預估 MLCC 佔比將自 2026 年底的 54%上升至 2027 年
的 57%，貢獻營收 YoY+49.7%；固態電容與電解電容雖然分別由 25%下滑至 24%與
9%下滑至 8%，但以貢獻營收衡量，YoY 仍分別增長 44.7%與 34.91%。儘管通路商
的毛利率較其他被動元件製造廠的毛利低，但在 2027 年預期 AI 占比將可望達 60%，
因此仍有望藉由調整旗下客戶去優化產品組合，且代理的原廠漲價也有機會帶動通路
價差增加，故 2027 年毛利率有望再次上揚。 
預估 2027 年 EPS 14.37 元，給予買進評等，目標價 172 元 
投 研 部 預 估 日 電 貿 2026 年 /2027 年 營 收 213.24 億元/304.43 億 元 ，
YoY+35.58%/+42.76%；毛利率為 19.99%/22.15%，YoY+3.6ppts/2.2ppts；稅後
EPS 9.80 元/14.37 元，目前 PER 約在 13.7X/9.2X，參考過去五年 PER 區間大部分在
10X~18X，目前股價以 2027 年衡量低於區間下緣，考量公司獨家代理的多項高階產
品因供需持續失衡而有望漲價，使 AI 佔比持續上升，故給予買進評等，但下修目標價
至 172 元(2027F PE 12X)。

個股報告

聯茂(6213) 
營運續強，逢低布局 
投資評等 目標價 
買進 $557 
前次投資建議：2026.09.08 
「買進」，目標價: $757

報告日期：2026.11.24 研 究 員：邱岳霖

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 報價續揚、產能擴張、新原料認證，2026年 EPS 22.8元。 
➢ 未來三年持續擴產，漲價與產品組合轉佳，將帶動利潤顯著提升，2027年 EPS 42.9 元，考
量市場整體利率提升不利高估值，故兆豐投顧調降目標價至 557元(13X 27(F)PER)，不過股
價先前已過度反應市場利空，潛在上漲空間 42.5%，投資評等維持「買進」。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/11/23 收盤價： 391.00 ➢ 展望2026年，隨著原物料供需吃緊與成本上升，公司
將持續調漲報價因應，同時預計於26Q4玻纖布供給有
部分改善，有助於稼動率提升，在漲價效益完整反映與
產品組合逐步改善下，且考量公司Q3財報優於預期，
同時上修Q4財測目標，故兆豐投顧上修聯茂2026年稅
後淨利83億元，YoY +449.8%，EPS 22.8元。

➢ 展望後市，聯茂持續在未來三年擴產，產能將由今年底
570萬張進一步提升至2029年910萬張，產能增加約
60%，且不排除進一步加碼擴張產能，主要鎖定AI 
Server與低軌衛星需求，雖近期市場雜音不斷，不過公
司一大CSP客戶需求已明確訂定2028年隻數量，同時
一般型伺服器CPU平台由M6升級M7，低軌衛星材料也
將明年開始貢獻營收，預期營運將持續高速增長；毛利
率方面，在AI Server PCB層數大幅提升，且新平台啟
動帶動高階材料需求進一步擴大，有利整體ASP提升，
輔以漲價帶動營收與利潤持續改善，考量26H2營運優
預期，上修兆豐投顧預估聯茂2027年營收730億元，
YoY +36.5%，毛利率39.8%，年增10.7ppt，稅後淨利
155.9億元，YoY +87.7%，EPS 42.9元(原估30.3元)。
考量市場整體利率提升不利高估值，故兆豐投顧調降目
標價至557元(13X 27(F)PER)，不過股價先前已過度反
應市場利空，潛在上漲空間42.5%，投資評等維持「買

個股報告

營 運 分 析

聯茂為國內第二大 CCL 供應商  
聯茂(6213)成立於 1997年，2008年掛牌上市，總部位於新竹縣新埔鎮，最
大股東為半導體廠穩懋。公司為全球前十大銅箔基板(CCL)廠、國內第二大
CCL供應商，同時是通用型伺服器 CCL的主力供應商，在 AMD平台市占率
逾六成、Intel 平台約三至四成。主力產品涵蓋銅箔基板(CCL)、玻纖布膠片
(Prepreg)、多層壓合基板及軟性基材，近年並將觸角延伸至 HDI 板材、IC
載板用材料與散熱材料，積極卡位 AI 伺服器、低軌衛星地面設備等高階應
用，作為公司下一階段成長動能。

生產基地涵蓋台灣新竹，中國江西、無錫、東莞、廣州以及泰國廠。銅箔基
板月產能於 2025 年底約 540 萬張，隨新產能陸續開出，預計 2026 年底增
至約 570 萬張，並規劃 2027 年底達 630 萬張、2028 年底 825 萬張、2029
年底逾 900 萬張，新增產能主要鎖定 M7 以上高階料號以因應 AI 伺服器需
求。2026應用別營收結構以基礎建設(通用及 AI 伺服器、網通設備等)為主，
占比約 73%，其次為車用電子 14%、消費性電子 8%與智慧型手機 5%。

- 4 - 
個股報告

營運持續衝高，毛利率大幅度跳升，獲利遠優預期，26Q3 EPS 8.4元 
聯茂(6213)公告 26Q3 財報：營收 156.17 億元，QoQ +35.5%/YoY 
+102.2%，優於預期，係因通用型伺服器新平台放量帶動高階材料佔比同步
墊高，同時原物料緊缺報價持續上揚，毛利率 34.3%，季增 12.3ppt，年增
20.6ppt，持續創高，優於研究部預期之 28%，亦優於公司展望，主要受到
玻纖布、銅箔原物料緊缺持續支撐漲價空間，稅後淨利 30.58 億元，QoQ 
+139.1%/YoY +841.9%，EPS 8.4元，遠優於研究部預估之 4.4元。

報價續揚、產能擴張、新原料認證，2026年 EPS 22.8元 
展望 2026年，隨著原物料供需吃緊與成本上升，公司將持續調漲報價因應，
同時預計於 26Q4 玻纖布供給有部分改善，有助於稼動率提升，在漲價效益
完整反映與產品組合逐步改善下，且考量公司 Q3 財報優於預期，同時上修
Q4 財測目標，故兆豐投顧上修聯茂 2026 年稅後淨利 83 億元，YoY 
+449.8%，EPS 22.8元(原估 19元)。

未來三年持續擴產，漲價與產品組合轉佳，將帶動利潤顯著提升，2027 年
EPS 42.9 元 
展望後市，聯茂持續在未來三年擴產，產能將由今年底 570萬張進一步提升
至 2029年 910萬張，產能增加約 60%，且不排除進一步加碼擴張產能，主
要鎖定 AI Server 與低軌衛星需求，雖近期市場雜音不斷，不過公司一大 CSP
客戶需求已明確訂定 2028年隻數量，同時一般型伺服器 CPU平台由 M6升
級 M7，低軌衛星材料也將明年開始貢獻營收，預期營運將持續高速增長；
毛利率方面，在 AI Server PCB層數大幅提升，且新平台啟動帶動高階材料
需求進一步擴大，有利整體 ASP提升，輔以漲價帶動營收與利潤持續改善，
考量 26H2營運優預期，上修兆豐投顧預估聯茂 2027年營收 730億元，YoY 
+36.5%，毛利率 39.8%，年增 10.7ppt，稅後淨利 155.9億元，YoY +87.7%，
EPS 42.9元(原估 30.3元)。

## Document 217: [005380] 현대차(005380) 26.3Q Review_ 이제 올라가야 할 때현대차(005380) 26.3Q Review_ 이제 올라가야 할 때26.3Q Review_ 이제 올라가야 할 때 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005380] 현대차(005380) 26.3Q Review_ 이제 올라가야 할 때현대차(005380) 26.3Q Review_ 이제 올라가야 할 때26.3Q Review_ 이제 올라가야 할 때 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

2026.10.30   
현대차 (005380)

26.3Q Review: 이제 올라가야 할 때 Company Brief

본업 Review: 26.3Q 매출액 45.1조원, 영업이익 2.6조원  
현대차는 26.3Q 매출액 45.1원 (-3.4% YoY, -8.3% QoQ), 영업이익 2.6조원 (+5.5% 
YoY, -6.1% QoQ, +5.9% OPM) 등의 실적을 시현했다 [iM증권 추정치 매출액: 
45.3조원, 영업이익: 2.4조원]. 매출액은 시장 컨센서스에 부합했고, 영업이익의 경우 
시장 컨센서스였던 2.4조원을 소폭 상회했다. 주요 구성 요소 별 내용은 아래와 같다.  
① P: 26.3Q HEV 판매 대수는 16.6만대를 기록하며 전사 판매 대비 비중 
18.2% 달성 (+18.8% YoY, -11.1% QoQ). 미국의 경우 국내 공장 생산 
감소에 따른 수출 부진으로 도매 판매는 소폭 감소했으나, 판매 비중은 
25.2%로 전반적인 믹스는 직전 분기와 유사. 26.3Q 평균환율이 직전 분기 
대비 크게 하락했으나, 전년 동기 기저 탓에 오히려 + 환율 효과 관찰됨. 
② Q: 26.3Q 도매 판매 대수는 91.8만대로 전년 동기 대비 11.5% 감소 (이하 
YoY 기준, 미국: -10.0%, 유럽: -18.1% 국내: -27.2% 인도: +18.3%). 
26.2Q 안전 공업 화재에 이어 26.3Q에 발생한 파업의 영향으로 생산 
대수는 감소. 국내 공장에서의 생산 차질은 내수 판매에 더해 수출 감소, 즉 
전체적인 판매 축소로 연결됨. 26.4Q부터 생산 관련 노이즈는 발생하지 
않을 전망. 이에 더해 아반떼, 투싼 등의 신차 판매 효과를 통한 반등 가능.  
③ C: 26.3Q 관세 비용은 직전 분기와 유사한 수준으로 발생한 것으로 파악 
(25.2Q 0.8조원, 25.3Q 1.8조원, 25.4Q 1.5조원, 26.1Q 0.8조원, 26.2Q 
0.9조원). 이란 사태에 따른 주요 원자재 가격 상승 압력은 계속 존재하나, 
내부 원가 절감 정책으로 약 5,000억원의 원가 상승분을 상쇄. 현대차는 
26.4Q 원가 절감 정책의 효과가 더 크게 나타날 것이라고 언급. 26.3Q 
기말 환율이 전년 동기 대비 급락함에 따라 판매보층충당부채전입액 또한 
감소 (26.3Q 매출원가, 판매관리비 각 전년 동기 대비 1.7%, 19.2% 감소). 
금융 부문의 경우 26.3Q 매출액 9.1조원 (+27.8% YoY), 영업이익 4,710억원 (-
18.2% YoY, -37.9%) 등의 실적을 기록했다. 완성차 판매가 전반적으로 
위축되었음에도 외형 성장을 이어갔으나, 금리 상승에 따른 이자 비용 등 전반적인 
조달 관련 비용이 증가했다. 일부 EV를 중심으로 중고차 가격이 소폭 하락한 점 또한 
HCA 등 주요 부문 이익 감소의 배경이다. 26.3Q 현대차 순이익은 1,932억원을 
기록했는데, 이는 영업외손익이 전년 동기 789억원에서 229억원으로 감소했기 
때문이다. 현대차는 주요 환율 하락에 따른 외화환산손실 증가의 영향이라고 밝혔다. 
1) 생산 단에서의 노이즈가 연속해 발생하며 내수 판매 및 수출 감소가 불가피했으나, 
2) 원자재 가격 상승 영향을 내부 원가 절감 정책을 통해 상쇄했고, 3) 주요 
평균환율이 하락했으나 기말환율 또한 급락하며 판매보증충당부채전입액 감소 효과가 
나타나 시장 기대를 소폭 상회하는 수익성이 관찰되었다는 것이 26.3Q 실적 총평이다. 
현대차는 2026년 도매 판매 가이던스를 416만대에서 400만대로 하향 조정했다. 
26.3Q 누적 도매 판매 대수는 288만대이기에 하향 조정은 불가피했다. 단 신규 
가이던스에 따르면 26.4Q 판매 목표는 약 110만대 수준이라는 것인데, 아반떼, 투싼, 
주요 모델 페이스리프트, GV80 HEV 등의 신차 효과가 얼마나 나타날지가 관건일 
것이다 (이하 분기 도매 판매, 25.1Q 100.1만대, 25.2Q 105.7만대, 25.3Q 103.8만대).

### Page 2

현대차 (005380)
2
미래 사업 업데이트: 자율주행은 계획대로, Atlas는 조금씩 변신중
26.3Q 컨퍼런스 콜에서 특별히 SDV~Boston Dynamics 관련 업데이트는 확인되지
않았으나 여러 매체를 통해 파악된 현대차그룹의 미래 사업 진행 상황은 다음과 같다.
① SDV/자율주행: 데이터 플라이 휠에 대한 중요성 강조. 27년 말부터 광주
권역 자율주행 실증 사업에 투입되는 차량은 Atria AI 활용. SDV 플랫폼이
적용된 차량 양산 시작은 29년. 양산 SDV 출하량 증가함에 따라 29년부터
GPU 5만개 이상으로 구성되는 새만금 데이터센터를 자체 운영을 목표함.
박민우 포티투닷 사장 겸 AVP 본부장이 부임한 이후 Tesla, nVIDIA 출신의
개발 인력을 충원 중인 상황. 26.05 그랜저 페이스리프트 모델에 플레오스
탑재 시작. 26.09부터 판매되고 있는 신형 아반떼에도 플레오스 탑재 완료.

② Boston Dynamics: 10.8 Rohit Prasad를 CEO로 선임. Rohit Prasad은
아마존에서 Alex 개발에 중추적인 역할을 수행했던 인물. 현대차그룹에서
직접 하드웨어 개발 및 양산을 챙기고, 소프트웨어에 역량을 높이겠다는
행보로 판단. 26.10 Atlas의 신규 덱스트러스를 공개했는데, 기존 제품 대비
자유도가 크게 증가 (7DoF→14DoF). 순수 직구동 방식을 여전히
고수함.10.27 Boston Dynamics가 주요 부품 업체에게 Atlas 1만대 물량의
부품 PO를 곧 발송할 것이라는 기사가 보도됨. 28년까지 연 3만대 CAPA의
공장 완공이라는 기존 계획 대비 양산 및 개발 속도가 빨라진다는 시그널임.
현대차그룹의 미래 사업 업데이트를 또 찾아볼 수 있을 가장 빠른 이벤트는 CES
2027이다. 다만 CES 2027에는 현대차가 아닌 그룹사가 참여함에 따라 해당 행사에서

유의미한 업데이트 내용은 찾아보기 힘들 것이며 이에 중장기 관점의 접근이 필요하다.
시장의 우려가 반영된 주가 하락: 빨라질 속도와 올라올 본업에 주목
현대차 주가는 26.2H 접어들며 크게 하락하고 있고, CES 2026 전후의 상승폭을 사실상
모두 반납했다 (연초 기준 -1.3%, 26.2H 이후 -41.7%). 또한 최근 시장 반등
흐름에서도 사실상 소외되고 있다. 현대차를 바라보는 투자자들의 우려는 아래와 같다.
첫째, CID 전후로 Boston Dynamics 관련 업데이트가 너무 부족하다는 것이다. 26.08
현대차 CID 행사를 앞두고 많은 투자자들은 빅테크 업체들의 지분 투자 및 Atlas
개발과 관련해 새로운 내용을 기대한 것이 사실이다. 이런 상황에서 유럽, 내수 등 주요
지역 에서의 판매 업황 또한 좋지 않다 보니, 시장의 실망은 더욱 컸다. 전술한 것과
같이 CES 2027에 현대차가 참여하지 않는다는 점에서 내년에 대한 기대도 크지 않다.

둘째, 내수와 유럽 업황이 그룹사 대비 너무 좋지 못하다는 점이다. 26.3Q 현대차 내수,
유럽 도매 판매는 전년 동기 대비 각 27.2%, 18.1% 감소했다. 반면 26.3Q 기아 내수,
유럽 도매 판매는 전년 동기 대비 각 2.2%, 18.7% 증가했다. 특히 유럽에서의 부진은
EV 라인업이 상대적으로 빈약하고, 중국 업체와의 경쟁이 심화되고 있다는 구조적
문제에서 출발하기에, 투자자들은 26년을 넘어 27, 28년의 성장까지 우려하고 있다
단 우리는 Boston Dynamics의 Atlas는 양산이라는 방향성이 설정된지 오래되지
않았다는 점을 기억해야 한다. 초기 단계부터 양산을 위해 개발된 Tesla Optimus 대비
개발 속도가 빠를 수는 없다. 1~2만대 수준의 데모 제품이 확보되고, 이를 바탕으로
데이터 수집이 일정 부분 확보되는 이후부터 관련 사업 확대 속도는 빨라 질것이다.

취약 지역 판매 업황의 경우에도 내수를 시작으로 점진적으로 개선될 것이다. 생산 차질
외에도 Tesla 및 중국 OEM들의 시장 침투가 우려됨은 분명하지만, 주력 차종 중에서도
국내 판매 볼륨이 큰 아반떼와 투싼의 신차 효과는 분명 기대되는 지점이다. 유럽에서의
반등은 시간이 필요할 것이나, 일단 아이오닉 3가 26.4Q부터 본격적으로 판매될
예정이다. 단 이는 큰 변동 없이 IAA가 2028년부터 시행된다는 전제에 근거하고 있다.
현대차 목표주가 600,000원 유지: 여전한 Multiple 할증 요인
현대차 목표주가를 600,000원으로 유지한다. 26년 EPS 기준 6~8배 수준의 P/E
Multiple을 부여받고 있는 Peer Group들은 중국 시장 M/S 축소로 수익성이 급락하고
있고, 미래 사업 경쟁력은 여전히 전무하다. 현대차의 Multiple 할증 요인은 충분하다.

그림16. IAA 법안 개요 
 `  
자료: iM증권 리서치본부

그림17. 유럽 생산 OEM 생산원가 추이 
 `  
자료: iM증권 리서치본부

IAA는 EU판 IRA로 볼 수 있음. 현대차그룹의 경우 현대차 (터키, 체코)와 기아 (슬로바키아) 모두 유렵 현지
생산 라인을 이미 확보했고, 현대모비스를 중심으로 한 부품 자급률 비중 이미 높음. 또한 주요 부품에 있어
FTA 체결국의 원산지를 EU산과 동일하게 적용하는 규정 존재. 실제 발효까지 큰 변수가 없다면 국내 완성차
업체의 경우 구조적으로 수혜 클 것. 단 중국 업체들에 대한 명시적 배제 규정이 없다는 점은 한계 중 하나. 
IAA 주요 조항 주요 내용
적용 대상
공공조달 차량, 공적 지원(구매 보조금 등)을 받는 차량, 법인차가 대상임. 전기
차, PHEV, 수소차에 적용됨. 2028년부터 회원국은 무공해 및 저공해이면서 EU
산인 차량이 아니면 법인차를 지원할 수 없음. 판매 금지가 아니라 수요 측 혜택
을 역내 생산과 연동하는 구조
역내 부품 요건
1단계(발효 6개월 후)는 EU 조립, 배터리 제외 부품가치 70% EU산, 셀 포함
배터리 핵심부품 3개 EU산을 요구함. 2단계(1단계 3년 후)는 배터리 핵심부품
5개(셀, 양극재,BMS 포함)와 전동 파워트레인·전장 가치 50% EU산이 추가됨.
소형차 특례
전장 4.2m 이하 전기 승용차가 대상임. EU 조립에 더해 부품가치 70%와
배터리 핵심부품 3개 중 하나만 충족하면 됨. 충족 시 CO2 규제상 1.3대로 인정
됨(2025~34년).
FDI 조건 1억 유로 초과 투자가 대상이며, 지분 49% 상한, EU 인력 50% 이상, EU산 투 입재 30% 이상 등의 조건이 붙음.
FTA·관세동맹국 예외
FTA나 관세동맹을 맺은 국가의 부품은 EU산과 동등한 부품으로 인정되며,
정부조달협정(GPA) 가입국도 일부 조항에 포함됨. 단 최종 조립국까지 인정되
는지는 불명확하며, 조문상으로는 EU, EEA 역내 조립만 인정되는 것으로 해석
됨. 따라서 한국산 부품은 EU산으로 인정 가능하나, 한국이나 튀르키예에서 조
립한 완성차는 요건 미충족 가능성이 높음.
FTA 예외 배제 조건
집행위는 위임법으로 특정국을 동등 대상에서 제외할 수 있음. 해당국이 EU 기업
에 내국민 대우를 하지 않는 경우, 공급망 의존도나 공급 안보 위협이 있는 경우,
해당 협정의 예외 조항에 해당하는 경우가 배제 사유임. 중국은 FTA가 없어 애초
에 대상이 아님.
2020 2021 2022 2023
대당 생산원가 30,683 32,029 35,079 38,140
대당 판매가격 34,404 27,557 31,244 33,560
1인당 인건비 70,981 74,904 78,348 88,198
통상적으로 중국 OEM의 생산 원가 (중국 생산 가정)은 글로벌 OEM 대비 30% 낮다고 추정됨. BYD의 경우 돌핀 등과 같
은 C 세그먼트 차량에서의 유럽 수익성이 낮지만, A~B 세그먼트 급의 돌핀 서프의 경우 매우 높은 수익성 시현. 이는 유럽
시장 내의 A~B 세그먼트 경쟁 강도가 낮다는 점에서 기인. 즉 100% 수준의 관세가 부과되지 않는다면 BYD의 가격 경쟁
력은 유지되는 것. 따라서 EU는 2,000유로 수준의 중국 OEM의 대당 이익을 상쇄하기 위해 이에 준하는 수준으로 지급되
는 EV 구매 보조금 및 법인 차량 세제혜택을 검토 중인 것이고, 이를 IAA에 담아낼지가 핵심.
유럽 생산 OEM 생산원가 및 판매가격 추이

그림23. 26.3Q 국내 완성차 실적 Summary

자료: iM증권 리서치본부

현대차 미주: 펠리세이드 리콜 이슈 존재하나, 메타플랜트 및 기존 앨러배마 공
장 HEV 생산을 통한 시장 진출 속도 빨라질 것으로 전망
인도: GM 탈레가온 공장 인수를 통한 생산량 확대에 나설 예정이고, 이
를 통한 인도 내수 수요 대응 및 글로벌 신흥 시장 진출 또한 목표
유럽: 기아 대비 EV 라인업의 폭에 열위에 있으나, 캐스퍼 EV, EV 3를
통해 EV 수요 회복 국면을 앞두고 있는 유럽 시장 대응에 나설 예정
기아 미주: 텔룰라이드 HEV 출시를 통해 Mid-Size  HEV 수요 대응 이뤄지
고 있음. 스포티지 및 셀토스 등 HEV를 통한 기존 차급 대응 강화 전망
인도: 2030년까지 10개 차종 출시 목표하고 있고, 이를 통해 2030년
41만대 도매 판매, 시장 점유율 7.6% 달성 가이던스를 제시함
유럽: EV2 출시를 통한 EV 라인업 완성도 높임. 소형 해치백 차량과 신
규 HEV 차량 출시, 그리고 PBV 판매 본격화를 통한 초과 성장 목표

당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

2 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports.1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.

MARKET VIEW
Europe’s Electoral Cycle and Market Risks
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
As midterm elections approach for the U.S. next month, voters across Europe will also
enter their own election season starting this November and continuing throughout
2027. Over the next year, 52% of the total EU population, representing 46% of EU GDP
across eight countries, will go to the polls, including voters in three of its four largest

economies (Exhibit 3). Having announced snap general elections earlier this month,
Spain will vote for members of its lower and upper house on November 29. A series of
parliamentary elections are then due for 2027 in Estonia (by March 7), Finland (on April
18), Greece (by July 25), Slovakia (by September 28), Poland (by November 11) and Italy
(by December 22). And perhaps most consequentially France will stage Presidential
elections on April 18, with a potential second round of voting set for May 2.

For the EU as a whole, the significance of these coming elections will be the potential
for a shift toward more populist, domestically oriented policy priorities from leading
opposition candidates (particularly in France, Spain and Poland) which could obstruct the
process of closer EU integration. In the nearer term, adoption of the new seven-year
(2028-2034) EU budget due by the end of 2027 could be impeded. And over the longer
term, renewed efforts toward an EU-wide capital market union to deepen and attract

more inward investment into European markets could also be further delayed.
At the country level, investors have seen bond yields rise globally in 2026 on the back of
higher inflation and concerns over fiscal sustainability. The debt question has arguably
been most pressing in parts of Europe, especially given the likelihood that upcoming
elections make for domestic increases in government largesse. And of the eight
countries going to the polls over the next year, France remains the biggest source of

concern. Interest rate spreads on French 10-year sovereign bonds have overtaken those
in both Greece and Italy this year (Exhibit 4), driven by investor concerns over public
debt. The 2027 government budget unveiled earlier this month projected a deficit of
5.0% of GDP (well in excess of the 3% specified in the EU Stability and Growth Pact),
but remained insufficiently expansive to nullify the risk that the current government
could be unseated later this year if it is forced through the fragmented parliament. This

comes on top of an existing debt load of close to 120% of GDP and a halving of official
2026 growth forecasts from 1.0% at the start of the year to 0.5% in September. In
short, all four variables in France’s debt sustainability equation currently stand as major
headwinds, and the two leading candidates in the 2027 election have shown limited
appetite for fiscal consolidation over the coming years.
Exhibit 3: Europe's Upcoming Elections Represent Around Half of EU GDP and Population.

Investment Implications
We remain cautious on European
markets overall, despite seeing
pockets of strength in selected
areas such as defense, data center
infrastructure and renewable energy.
On top of growing uncertainty
from the upcoming electoral cycle

and interest rate outlook, rising
competition and slowing demand
from China continue to challenge
key segments of the Consumer
Discretionary sector. And limited
direct AI concentration also leaves
Europe under-exposed to the key
growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and
emerging Asia.

Intervention from the ECB under its 2022 bond purchase program is also unlikely at this

stage given that the spread widening in France has not appeared either disorderly or
unwarranted. Meanwhile, conditions could potentially be aggravated by expected ECB rate
hikes in response to still above-target inflation across the eurozone. Indeed recent moves
to tighten monetary policy from a number of major central banks have been read most
hawkishly in Europe. Most of the increase in European bond yields has come from higher
inflation breakevens as opposed to real yields in the U.S. and Japan. And despite some

paring back of tightening expectations in the wake of recent bond volatility, the market is
still pricing three more policy rate increases over the next 12 months.
Euro depreciation and an emerging shift toward more protectionist trade policy with China
could further complicate the ECB policy outlook. China posted a record $35B trade surplus,
according to Eurostat, with the EU in September (alongside its $938B total surplus for the
first three quarters of 2026), and the two markets have agreed to reduce the EU bilateral

deficit by capping imports of hybrid cars for four years. Hybrids account for less than 2%
of total EU imports from China, but this could potentially mark the start of a broader shift
toward more trade protection in Europe with its largest foreign supplier which risks adding
further upward pressure to cost-push inflation. And though the leading candidates in the
French election may favor resuming oil and gas imports from Russia, Europe also remains

among the most at risk from the global energy shock. Indeed some 56% of the region’s
crude oil consumption is in the form of diesel product according to the International Energy
Agency, more than twice the U.S. share.
The 2- to 10-year eurozone yield curve has already flattened significantly since the start
of the year. And at just over 30 basis points as of mid-October, it remains flatter than
its equivalents in the U.S., UK and Japan. Outside Energy and Technology, Financials have

been among the strongest-performing sectors in Europe this year. But additional ECB
rate hikes could lead to further curve flattening, compressing bank net interest margins
and contributing to slower growth—particularly given that rate increases are coming in
response to supply-driven inflation rather than reflecting stronger economic activity.
We nonetheless continue to see pockets of strength in European markets. Growing
defense budgets should remain a tailwind for Industrials as national governments look to

meet their North Atlantic Treaty Organization targets over the next several years. And the
sector should be further supported by indirect exposure to AI investment through electric
cabling, power infrastructure and building materials for data centers. Leaders in renewable
energy equipment and services are also likely to be beneficiaries of grid upgrades and
broadening electrification globally, particularly given elevated fossil fuel prices. But our view

on the region remains cautious overall. On top of growing uncertainty from the upcoming
electoral cycle and interest rate outlook, rising competition and slowing demand from
China continue to challenge key segments of Europe’s Consumer Discretionary sector.
Limited direct AI concentration in technology hardware, cloud services and application
software also leaves Europe underexposed to the key growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and emerging Asia.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Cautious Optimism Entering Fiscal Year 2027
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
“A renewed focus on rebuilding fiscal space is urgent, given elevated debt, higher
borrowing costs, multiplying demands on public budgets and heightened uncertainty.” So
warned the IMF about the global fiscal outlook last week in its updated World Economic

Outlook report. Much is at stake for developed nations in Europe and Asia facing high
existing debt, unfavorable demographics and more modest growth.
For the U.S., between structurally higher mandatory outlays, the administration’s $1.5T
proposed defense package, and the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield sitting above 5%, spending
is likely headed higher. We continue to fade concerns of an impending U.S. debt crisis,
however, largely because economic growth continues to surprise to the upside. Expanding

output is good news for the debt-to-GDP ratio, which remains a touch below 100%.
Indeed, rather than being led higher on account of un-anchored inflation expectations, the
10-year U.S. Treasury yield has repriced to reflect better-than-expected nominal growth
(Exhibit 5A). Higher yields in the face of slowing growth, meanwhile, would be a different
story, not only for the fiscal outlook (worse) but also for U.S. equity valuations (lower).
Of course, after the U.S. accumulated another $2T deficit in FY 2026,2 “rebuilding fiscal

space” in the IMF’s words will be politically unpopular and mathematically challenging.
In our view, the biggest near-term tail risk would be a surprise move lower in growth as
budget deficits at 6% of GDP in good times leave little room for bad times. This isn’t our
base case given robust consumer spending per our internal credit and debit card data and
ongoing investment in the AI buildout and beyond. We expect these two pillars to sustain
growth in 2027.

The other good news is that, contrary to concerns, demand for Treasurys remains robust.
Yields are attractive—that was the message from October’s successful $39B 10-year
Treasury note auction. At the same time, U.S. debt boasts an array of buyers: domestic
households, the Federal Reserve, mutual funds and exchange-traded funds, money market
funds, pension funds, foreign central banks, private investors overseas, and more. Per
Exhibit 5B, whereas foreign official investors once dominated some 40% of the U.S.

Treasury market during the 2008/2009 Great Financial Crisis, their share has fallen to
12% while domestic private investors now account for a majority of holdings. Translated:
demand is more diverse than most appreciate.
Exhibit 5: Higher Yields Meet Shifting Composition of U.S. Treasury Demand.
A) Higher 10-year Yield Consistent with Strong Nominal Growth Environment. B) Domestic Private Investors Now Dominate U.S. Treasury Market.

Investment Implications
In our view, higher nominal and real
yields present a compelling entry point
for investors seeking income and can
provide cushion in the face of higher
rate volatility. We expect demand for
U.S. assets to remain healthy and favor
the U.S. over developed economies in
Europe facing more modest growth and
fiscal instability.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.

oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.

of all investors.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

## Document 309: CTCP Vàng Bạc Đá Quý Phú Nhuận - Cập nhật ĐHCĐ bất thường 21_10_2026 (2026-10-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/CTCP Vàng Bạc Đá Quý Phú Nhuận -  Cập nhật ĐHCĐ bất thường 21_10_2026 (2026-10-22).pdf
Report source: KBSV (Company)
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

CTCP VÀNG BẠC ĐÁ QUÝ PHÚ NHUẬN (PNJ)  
Cập nhật ĐHĐCĐ bất thường 21/10/2026

Tái cơ cấu để xử lý áp lực thanh khoản  
— ĐHĐCĐ bất thường PNJ ngày 21/10/2026 đã thông qua điều chỉnh kế hoạch kinh 
doanh và phương án phát hành riêng lẻ để bổ sung vốn tái cấu trúc. Ban lãnh đạo đặt 
ra các vấn đề trọng yếu là đáp ứng nhu cầu thanh khoản và chuẩn bị nguồn lực để thực 
hiện các cam kết thu đổi đã tích lũy trong quá khứ. Bên cạnh đó, các cổ đông tham dự 
cũng đặt các câu hỏi liên quan đến tình hình hoạt động qua các điểm chính (1) dòng 
tiền hoạt động kinh doanh; (2) diễn biến thị trường sau khi PNJ áp dụng chính sách thu 
đổi mới; (3) hiệu quả hoạt động sau tái cấu trúc.

Nội dung chính tại đại hội 
— Theo Ban lãnh đạo, thông tin liên quan đến P-Lab từ đầu tháng 7 khiến khách hàng gia 
tăng bán lại sản phẩm. Tổng giá trị thu đổi và mua lại trong tháng đạt gần 6,000 tỷ VND, 
so với khoảng 7,000 tỷ VND trong cả năm 2025. Theo BCTC riêng, nguồn tiền khả dụng 
giảm từ khoảng 3,700 tỷ VND cuối Q2/2026 xuống 770 tỷ VND cuối Q3/2026, đã bao 
gồm khoản vay 700 tỷ VND từ Chủ tịch HĐQT. Tỷ trọng kim cương và trang sức kim 
cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% lên 52%. 
— Q3/2026 ghi nhận lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND trước phần tăng trích lập dự phòng. Để giảm 
áp lực thanh toán, PNJ kéo dài thời gian chi trả đối với kim cương lên 120 ngày từ tháng 
07/2026. Thay đổi chính sách cũng ảnh hưởng đến niềm tin khách hàng và sức mua, cụ 
thể doanh thu và lợi nhuận gộp Q3/2026 đạt lần lượt 6,995/609 tỷ VND, giảm 14%/62% 
YoY. Chi phí bán hàng và quản lý giảm 13% YoY chưa đủ bù đắp mức giảm lợi nhuận 
gộp, khiến PNJ lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND, so với lãi 496 tỷ VND trong Q3/2025.

Biểu đồ 1. Kết quả kinh doanh theo quý  Biểu đồ 2. Diễn biến doanh thu thuần/ngày và doanh thu bán 
lẻ/ngày theo quý

### Page 2

2

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T 
 — PNJ nâng giả định tỷ lệ thu đổi trên giá trị thu đổi còn lại lên 80%, so với 28.8% đối với 
nhóm sản phẩm giai đoạn 2020–2026 trong ước tính tại BCTC bán niên 2026. Tổng dự 
phòng tổn thất dự kiến tăng từ 2,267 tỷ VND lên 7,071 tỷ VND. Kế hoạch điều chỉnh ghi 
nhận doanh thu 39,057 tỷ VND, lỗ trước thuế 6,059 tỷ VND và lỗ sau thuế 6,271 tỷ VND. 
Theo Ban lãnh đạo, LNST loại trừ dự phòng ước khoảng 800 tỷ VND, chủ yếu nhờ kết 
quả Q1/2026. Khoản dự phòng phản ánh tổn thất ước tính cho toàn bộ kịch bản, không 
đồng nghĩa toàn bộ giá trị sẽ được chi trả ngay trong kỳ. 
— Giá trị cam kết thu đổi tích lũy đối với kim cương và trang sức kim cương tại cuối 
Q3/2026 được PNJ ước tính khoảng 21,000 tỷ VND. Với hàng đã bán, doanh nghiệp dự 
kiến chuyển đổi quyền lợi khách hàng sang sản phẩm và các chương trình ưu đãi. Với 
sản phẩm bán mới, chính sách thu đổi định hướng theo giá thị trường tại thời điểm 
giao dịch, thay cho tỷ lệ cố định trên giá trị hóa đơn, nhằm hạn chế tiếp tục tích lũy 
nghĩa vụ tiềm tàng trong tương lai theo cơ chế cũ.

Phương án bổ sung vốn và tái cơ cấu hoạt động kinh doanh 
— Đại hội thông qua phương án chào bán tối đa 550 triệu cổ phiếu. Nguồn vốn dự kiến 
phân bổ 60% cho kế hoạch kinh doanh gắn với điều chỉnh chính sách thu đổi, 20% bổ 
sung vốn lưu động và phần còn lại bổ sung vốn lưu động và/hoặc trả vay ngắn hạn. 
Nhu cầu vốn Q4/2026 và Q1/2027 lần lượt khoảng 3,100 tỷ VND và 2,800 tỷ VND. Giá và 
khối lượng phát hành thực tế chưa được xác định tại đại hội.  
— Doanh nghiệp dự kiến đóng 25–30 cửa hàng và mở khoảng 10 cửa hàng (có chọn lọc) 
trong năm 2026, tương ứng giảm ròng 15–20 cửa hàng; đồng thời đặt mục tiêu giảm 
20–30% chi phí nhân sự. Cổ tức tiền mặt năm 2026 được điều chỉnh về 0%; cổ tức 2025 
về 10% mệnh giá và đã chi trả. 
— 100% mẫu kim cương được AIGS và Vinacontrol kiểm định độc lập là kim cương thiên 
nhiên, phù hợp thông số 4C. PNJ dự kiến trang bị máy kiểm định tại các cửa hàng trọng 
điểm để khách hàng kiểm tra trực tiếp.

Đánh giá của KBSV 
— KBSV cho rằng huy động vốn từ phát hành riêng lẻ là phương án phù hợp ở thời điểm 
hiện tại trong bối cảnh lãi suất tăng cao, giúp PNJ cân đối thanh khoản và duy trì hoạt 
động liên tục. Tuy nhiên, với khối lượng phát hành lớn và chưa có phương án chi tiết, 
doanh nghiệp sẽ cần thêm thời gian để hoàn thành phương án phát hành và cân đối 
giữa lợi ích của cổ đông và doanh nghiệp.  
— Nhu cầu yếu tại Trung Quốc và cạnh tranh từ kim cương nuôi cấy giá thấp tiếp tục gây 
áp lực lên giá kim cương tự nhiên, khiến PNJ gặp khó khăn trong việc tiêu thụ tồn kho 
nhóm kim cương  và gia tăng tổn thất khi mua lại theo chính sách cũ. Thực tế, tỷ trọng 
kim cương và trang sức kim cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% cuối Q2/2026 lên 
52% cuối Q3/2026, với giá trị ước khoảng 9,000 tỷ VND. KBSV cho rằng cần theo dõi (1) 
nhu cầu thị trường trong giai đoạn cuối năm; (2) tốc độ giảm tồn kho và dòng tiền thu 
về từ bán hàng; và (3) giá trị khách hàng bán lại. Diễn biến các yếu tố này sẽ quyết định 
khả năng thu hồi vốn, mức độ phục hồi biên lợi nhuận và nhu cầu trích lập bổ sung 
trong quá trình xử lý. 
— Ước tính bán niên sử dụng tỷ lệ tổn thất 20.3% đối với hàng bán trong giai đoạn 2020–
2026 dự kiến được thu mua lại, thấp hơn mức 28.9% trên các giao dịch mua lại thực tế 
từ 01/07–03/09/2026. Vì vậy, bên cạnh giả định thu đổi 80%, mức tổn thất khi xử lý 
hàng mua lại là yếu tố quan trọng cần theo dõi. Việc chuyển hạch toán dự phòng từ 
SG&A sang giá vốn tại kỳ soát xét bán niên cũng cho thấy áp lực biên lợi nhuận bị co 
hẹp trong quá trình tái cấu trúc về mặt kế toán.

## Document 330: First Impressions_ NZ retail trade, September quarter 2026 - New Zealanders fill up the car and head back to the mall (2026-11-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ retail trade, September quarter 2026 - New Zealanders fill up the car and head back to the mall (2026-11-25).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#retail #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ retail trade, September quarter 2026 - New
Zealanders fill up the car and head back to the mall
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:19 November 23 2026 Share
The September quarter saw a larger than expected rise in spending, including firmness in
core categories.
September quarter retail sales
Retail sales (volume of goods sold): +2.2% (Prev: -0.4%)

### Page 2

Westpac f/c: 1.4%, Market: +0.5% 
Core retail sales (volume of goods sold): 1.4% (Prev: 0.8%)

Nominal retail sales: 2.4% (Prev: 1.0%)

Year to September
Volume of goods sold: 3.5%
Nominal sales: 7.0%

New Zealanders fill up the car and head back to the mall.
The September quarter retail sales report showed a sharp recovery in spending. Nominal spending
was up 2.4% over the quarter, and the amount of goods that New Zealanders are actually taking home
was up a solid 2.2%.
Today’s result was above our top of market forecast for a 1.4% quarterly rise, and well above the
median market forecast for +0.5%.
While the strong September quarter result is welcome news, we are taking it with a grain of salt. Much
of the rise in spending was related to fuel spending, which was boosted by the fall in fuel prices over
the September quarter, and fuel prices have step higher since then.
However, even excluding fuel, we have seen firmness in spending appetites. Core spending (excl. fuel
and motor vehicles) was up 1.4% over the September quarter and is 4.7% higher than this time last
year. 
Looking at the breakdown of spending, we did see a rise in grocery purchases. Meanwhile, spending in
discretionary categories was mixed – there has been lower spending on items like furnishings and
hardware, while spending on hospitality, electronics and recreational items is up. Overall, were left with
a picture of gradually improving spending appetites.

Outlook
Today’s report reinforces our expectations that the economy is moving into stronger phase. We expect
that spending levels will continue to firm through the holiday season and 2027. However, that recovery
is still likely to be gradual due to the combination of ongoing high inflation, a soft labour market and
rising interest rates. We also expect the recovery will be uneven, with those regions with strong links to
the agricultural and tourism sectors outperforming metro regions, like Auckland and Wellington.

Implications for GDP growth
We’re forecasting GDP growth of 0.6% in the September quarter. Today’s result was above
expectations. We’ll take a closer look at how our forecast for GDP growth is shaping up over the next
couple of weeks as additional data on September quarter activity is released.

Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist

## Document 349: Is the economy ‘plucked’_ (2026-11-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Is the economy ‘plucked’_ (2026-11-25).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 31
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

A New Keynesian ‘plucking’ model of unemployment and inflation
PRELIMINARY AND INCOMPLETE
Anthony Brassil* and Callum Ryan*
Research Discussion Paper
20XX-YY
December 2025
*Economic Research Department
Reserve Bank of Australia
The views expressed in this paper are those of the authors and should not be attributed to the
Reserve Bank of Australia. Any errors are the sole responsibility of the authors.
Author: ryanca at domain rba.gov.au
External Communications: rbainfo@rba.gov.au

https://doi.org/10.47688/rdp20XX-YY

### Page 2

Abstract
We construct a New Keynesian model with search-and-matching frictions in the labour market that
can generate the nonlinear ‘plucking’ dynamics of unemployment in the data: unemployment tends to
spike upwards and then slowly decline. The model features endogenous layoffs, a ‘slippery’ job ladder,
downward nominal wage rigidity, and convex recruiting costs. It generates both the ‘deepness’ and
‘steepness’ asymmetries in unemployment dynamics, and broadly matches the response of labour-

market flows in a downturn. The model endogeneously generates a Phillips curve relationship that
is strongly nonlinear and state-dependent, and a short-run non-accelerating rate of unemployment
(NAIRU) that fluctuates asymmetrically over the business cycle. We also show that cyclical properties
(e.g. shock volatility, nominal rigidities, policy regime) can have a significant effect on the long-run
NAIRU via agents’ precautionary response to future uncertainty.

JEL Classification Numbers:
Keywords:

### Page 4

Figure 1: Unemployment rate in Australia
*Long-run NAIRU estimate from Ballantyne and Cusbert (2025).
1. Introduction
The unemployment rate in the data has strongly nonlinear dynamic (Figure 1). It spikes sharply in
occasional downturns, then declines slowly but persistently in recoveries.1 It is right-skewed in both
levels (i.e. asymmetric ‘deepness’) and in changes (i.e. asymmetric ‘steepness’) (Sichel 1993). Gross
flows between unemployment and employment display similar nonlinearities. Standard search-and-

matching models of the labour market struggle to generate these nonlinearities in unemployment
and labour-market flows (Ferraro 2023).
If this asymmetric pattern stems from frictions within the labour market, what implication do those
frictions have for the relationship between inflation and unemployment? Research using nonlinear
structural models to explore the asymmetric dynamics in the labour market tend to abstract from price
frictions and inflation dynamics (e.g. Dupraz, Nakamura and Steinsson 2025; Ferraro 2018; Petrosky-

Nadeau, Zhang and Kuehn 2018), while in the New Keynesian literature, the usual approach of
linearising equilibrium conditions excludes these nonlinear dynamics by assumption. The 2020s
inflation surge has reignited research into the nonlinearity of the Phillips curve, and the extent to which
this nonlinearity stems from frictions within the labour market (e.g. Benigno and Eggertsson 2023).
But this literature tends not to model the labour market in as much detail.

In this paper, we explore the extent to which a detailed search-and-matching model of the labour
market with pricing frictions can match the nonlinear dynamics in the labour market. We then use
the model to examine the implications of these nonlinearities for the Phillips curve relationship and
the non-accelerating rate of unemployment (NAIRU).
1 Friedman (1964) uses the ‘plucking’ description for the asymmetric way in which output seems to be occasionally ‘plucked’

below trend, before recovering back to trend.

### Page 5

2
We start with a general-equilibrium search-and-matching model similar to Bloesch, Lee and
Weber (2024), in which firms post wages and vacancies and set prices (subject to nominal rigidities
and recruiting costs), and workers search (while unemployed and on the job) and decide whether to
accept and quit jobs. Policy steers aggregate demand to balance inflation and unemployment targets.
We extend this model to include endogenous layoffs, downward nominal wage rigidity, and a ‘slippery’

job ladder (drawing on Dupraz et al (2025)).2 We find that these features - together with convex
recruiting costs - are collectively key to achieving both the asymmetric spikes in unemployment when
shocks occur and the slow speed in the subsequent recovery. The model is sufficiently detailed to
broadly match the dynamics of unemployment and gross flows, but tractable enough that we can
obtain the global nonlinear solution with rational expectations and aggregate uncertainty. This is

important because agents’ precautionary response to future uncertainty is a significant determinant
of outcomes, especially the long-run NAIRU.
We show that the model can generate substantial asymmetry in both ‘deepness’ and ‘steepness’
(comparable to or greater than in the data). The response of unemployment, job-finding, and
separations after a stylised negative demand shock broadly matches the response of these variables in
downturns over the past 50 years. Aggregate demand shocks have an especially asymmetric effect,

because the effect on inflation amplifies the effect of the downward nominal wage rigidity. When
supply shocks occur, the inflation response somewhat eases the downward nominal wage constraint.
The model endogenously generates a Phillips curve relationship that is strongly nonlinear and state-
dependent. Conditional on some initial state of the economy, the Phillips curve is much steeper for
low rates of unemployment. When the initial unemployment rate is higher, the Phillips curve shifts to

the right, and when the initial level of real wages is higher, it shifts upwards.
We can also use the model to simulate a ‘short-run NAIRU’, which is the unemployment rate consistent
with eliminating inflationary pressure from the labour market at some cyclical horizon (e.g. 1-2 years).
This short-run NAIRU varies asymmetrically with the state of the economy. It tends to rise when
unemployment is high, but also when unemployment has fallen rapidly (because real wages increase).

Finally, we can use the ‘stochastic steady state’ of the model to explore the determinants of the long-
run NAIRU, i.e. the unemployment rate consistent with stable inflation in the absence of shocks (but
where agents still expect that shocks may occur in the future). We find that when firms account for
the interaction between aggregate risk and asymmetric wage adjustment costs in their wage-setting
decisions, it generates a trade-off between unemployment variance and steady-state unemployment.

For example, if policy places greater weight on reducing the gap between unemployment and the
long-run NAIRU (instead of the gap between inflation and target), it tends to increase the long-run
NAIRU. This is because by mitigating fluctuations in labour demand, policy reduces the risk of hitting
downward nominal wage rigidities. Firms are then more willing to set higher real wages in steady
state, which reduces the optimal amount of employment in steady state.

The remainder of this introduction discusses the literature. Section 2 outlines the model. Section 3
evaluates the extent to which it can match the nonlinear dynamics in labour-market data. Section 4
considers the implications for the Phillips curve and NAIRU.
2 By ‘slippery’ job ladder, we mean that workers recently hired out of unemployed are much more likely to be laid off.

### Page 6

3
Literature review. The cyclical asymmetries in unemployment are established empirically by e.g.
Sichel (1993), McKay and Reis (2008), and Dupraz et al (2025). Ferraro (2023) analyses the ability of
the baseline Diamond-Mortensen-Pissarides (DMP) model to produce these asymmetries, and finds
that it cannot generate the ‘steepness’ asymmetry (i.e. right-skewness in unemployment changes).
Petrosky-Nadeau et al (2018) finds that the DMP model can generate the ‘deepness’ asymmetry given

large enough shocks.
Dupraz et al (2025) and Ferraro (2018) propose search-and-matching models that can generate
sharp spikes and slow declines in unemployment. We take several features from Dupraz et al (2025),
including downward nominal wage rigidity and a ‘slippery’ job ladder. Pizzinelli, Theodoridis and
Zanetti (2020) explore the state-dependence of unemployment fluctuations in a model with
endogenous layoffs and on-the-job search.3 Adjemian, Karamé and Langot (2021) estimate a

nonlinear model with endogenous separations, downward wage rigidity, worker heterogeneity and
segmented markets, and show that it can generate similar asymmetries in unemployment, hiring,
and separations to the data.
Our definition of a ‘short-run NAIRU’ is similar to the concept discussed by Hall and Kudlyak (n.d.), who
argue that the natural rate of unemployment may vary cyclically and track the actual unemployment
rate during recoveries. Hall and Kudlyak (2022) discusses the ability of DMP-style models to generate

the slow persistent recovery in unemployment that occurs after recessions. Fujita and Ramey (2007)
show that adding vacancy adjustment costs to a DMP model can produce a more sluggish response
for vacancies and hiring after a downturn, slowing down the recovery in unemployment. Mercan,
Schoefer and Sedláček (2024) suggest a congestion mechanism, whereby new hires and incumbent
workers are imperfect substitutes in production, which also propagates unemployment after a shock.

This literature above generally abstracts from price rigidities and inflation dynamics. There is a
substantial literature incorporating labour-market frictions into New Keynesian models (see e.g.
Blanchard and Galí 2010). Recent examples include Moscarini and Postel-Vinay (2023), Faccini and
Melosi (2025), and Bloesch et al (2024). These papers focus on the role of on-the-job search and wage
competition between firms in inflation dynamics. We adopt a similar model for wage setting, drawing

on the tractable formulation in Bloesch et al (2024). But these papers use linear approximations.
The literature on downward nominal wage rigidity explores the extent to which it generates a
nonlinear Phillips curve and asymmetric cyclical dynamics (see e.g. Kim and Ruge-Murcia 2009;
Abbritti and Fahr 2013; Daly and Hobijn 2014; Benigno and Antonio Ricci 2011; Schmitt-Grohé
and Uribe 2022; Benigno and Eggertsson 2023; Benigno and Eggertsson 2024). Lepetit (2020)

also constructs a New Keynesian model with asymmetric unemployment dynamics, although the
asymmetry stems from the convex relationship between the job-finding rate and unemployment.
This literature does not model the labour market in as much detail (e.g. endogenous layoffs, on-the-
job search, job ladder) and does not seek to match the ‘plucking’ property in unemployment to the
same extent.
3 Fujita and Ramey (2012) also analyse versions of the DMP model with endogenous separations and on-the-job search,

but their focus is not on asymmetries in unemployment rate dynamics

### Page 7

4
2. Model
The model consists of firms, workers, and a policy rule. We start by outlining the firms’ problem
and optimal choices. Firms’ optimal decision-making drives most of the model’s mechanics. We then
discuss workers and the policy regime, which are modelled more simply. 5 contains a full list of
equilibrium conditions, as well as details of the calibration and solution method.
2.1 Firms
Firms make decisions around hiring, separations, and price- and wage-setting.

Hiring: Firms choose how many vacancies to post, V i t . These vacancies generate Mi t = qtV i t matches,
where qt is determined by a matching function and depends on aggregate matching efficiency and
labour market tightness. A proportion 1 − ϕ E t of these matches are unemployed searchers (where ϕ E t
is the share of unemployed searchers in total searchers, weighted by search intensity).
Firms must screen matches. Then the individual productivities of employed matches are revealed.

Firms make offers to those with productivities above some threshold xi t , which is 1−F i t of them (where
F i t = F(xi t ) and F is the CDF of the individual productivity distribution). For unemployed matches,
firms have no information about their individual productivity and make offers to all of them. This
feature proxies for the idea that firms may be more uncertain about the capabilities of unemployed
applicants.
Matches with offers choose whether to accept them; Ace t (W i t ) and employed matches and Acu t (W i t )

of unemployed matches do so, where W i t is the real wage paid by the firm. These job-acceptance
probabilities are characterised in the description of workers below.
The number of new hires is given by
Hi t = ϕ E t Mi t (1 − F i t )Ace t (W i t ) + (1 − ϕ E t )Mi t Acu t (W i t ).
Define Hi,u t = (1 − ϕ E t )Mi t Acu t (W i t ) as the number of hires from unemployment.
Recruiting costs include a per-vacancy cost and a per-match ‘screening’ cost:

Separations: There are two types of separation: quits and layoffs. Each period, Qt (W i t ) of firms’
existing employees quit (either to unemployment or to another job). Just as firms’ hiring rate is

increasing in the real wage they offer (via the job-acceptance probabilities Ace t (W i t ) and Acu t (W i t )),
their quits rate is decreasing in the real wage by the firm. This function is characterised in the section
below on workers.
Workers’ probability of being laid off depends on whether they are insecurely or securely attached.
Insecurely attached employees are all those who have just been hired out of unemployed in
the previous period. Before aggregate shocks are revealed, some portion of new hires from

### Page 8

5
unemployment, δ u, (i.e. who were hired the previous period), draw a new productivity from
distribution G. Those whose productivities are less than some threshold xi,m t−1 are laid off, which
is Gi t−1 of them (where Gi t = G(xi,m t−1) and G is the CDF of the distribution).4 All insecurely attached
employees who are not laid off then because securely attached (together with all those who have
been employed for more than one period).
After aggregate shocks are revealed and quitting decisions made, δ of securely attached employees

draw a new individual productivity. Firms then decide to lay off those whose productivities are less
than some threshold xi t , which is F i t of them (where F i t = F(xi t ) and F is the CDF of the individual
productivity distribution). The layoff probability for securely attached employees will be lower than
for insecurely attached employees both because δ < δ u and the distribution F has less mass at low
values than G.

2.2 Workers
All workers who are unemployed when shocks are revealed search for jobs. A share λ EE of employed
workers also search. The total number of searchers is then λ EE ˜Nt−1 + 1 − ˜Nt−1, and the effective
share of employed searchers is

Those who enter unemployment after shocks are revealed, either because they are laid off or because

they quit to unemployment, do not search in that period.6
A share λ EU of employed workers consider quitting to unemployment.
6 This means that even if hiring costs went to zero, unemployment would remain above zero. If the newly unemployed
were allowed to search, this would raise the average number of searchers, especially in periods where layoffs are high.
Workers hired last period who are laid off before shocks are revealed can search. This is to avoid Hu t−1 or Nt−1 being a

state variable.

Workers’ only role in the model is to determine the labour supply schedule faced by firms. They do so
by determining the hiring and quits elasticities ε He t , ε Hu t and ε Q t via the functions Ace t (Wt ), Acu t (Wt )
and Q(Wt ).
